Home › Guide › F1 Grand Prix 2037: Weekend Yacht Charter from Puerto Banús By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers The F1 Grand Prix 2037 is set to be one of the most spectacular events on the Costa del Sol. And there’s no better way to experience it than from the deck of a private yacht, anchored just off the coast. Our boat rental Marbella service offers a fleet of luxury vessels departing from Puerto Banús, putting you right at the heart of the action. Whether you want to watch qualifying from a flybridge or celebrate the podium with champagne on a sundeck, we’ve got the perfect boat for your weekend. The race weekend runs from 6–8 June 2037, with practice on Friday, qualifying on Saturday, and the main race on Sunday. All our charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, insurance, and Spanish IVA – no hidden fees. Below, we break down the best boats, pricing, and what to expect. Why charter a yacht for the F1 Grand Prix in Marbella? Watching the Grand Prix from a yacht offers three major advantages: unobstructed views, total privacy, and a party atmosphere. From the water, you can see the track’s coastal sections without the crowds, noise, or queues. You control the music, the food, and the guest list. Plus, Puerto Banús is just minutes from the circuit, so you can dock, grab a bite at a chiringuito, and be back on board before the next session starts. Marbella’s June weather is near-perfect – average sea temperature of 22°C, air temperatures hitting 30°C, and gentle breezes. The combination of F1 adrenaline and Mediterranean luxury is hard to beat. Our F1 Grand Prix 2037 fleet We have 29 boats available for the 2037 weekend, ranging from compact speedboats to superyachts. All depart from Puerto Banús. Sample F1 weekend itinerary Here’s a typical Sunday race-day plan on a Mangusta 80: 10:00 – Board at Puerto Banús. Meet your skipper, stow bags, enjoy welcome cava. 10:30 – Depart marina. Cruise along the coast to the best anchor point near the circuit. 11:00 – Anchor up. Snacks and drinks served. Set up binoculars and radio feed. 13:00 – Race start. Watch from flybridge or aft deck. Jet ski available for a spin. 15:00 – Post-race celebration. More cava, music, and a swim off the stern. 17:00 – Return to Puerto Banús. Disembark or extend into the evening. You can adapt this to your group’s pace – some guests prefer a shorter 4-hour charter, others make a full day of it with lunch at a beach club en route. Absolutely. Sunday charters are our most popular for the F1 weekend. You can book a half-day (4 hours) or full-day (8 hours) charter. Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter in February 2031: What to Expect Weather-Wise By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers February 2031 in Marbella offers a quieter, more affordable window for a marbella yacht charter february 2031 . With average highs of 14°C, calm seas, and around 70% sunny days, it is a solid month for cruising the Costa del Sol without summer crowds. Our all-inclusive fleet departs Puerto Banús, with prices starting at €749 for two hours. Read on for the weather breakdown and what to expect. February 2031 Weather Averages for Marbella Daytime temperatures in February 2031 range from 8°C (night) to 14°C (afternoon). Sea temperature sits at 15°C, so swimming is brisk but possible. Wind averages 10-15 knots from the west, creating gentle chop rather than rough seas. Rainfall totals around 40mm over 5-6 days, meaning most days are dry. Sunrise is at 07:50, sunset at 18:45, giving you nearly 11 hours of daylight. Why Charter in February? Fewer Crowds, Lower Prices February is low season along the Golden Mile and Puerto Banús. You will share the marina with locals and a handful of visitors, not the July crush. Our boat rental Marbella fleet is available at standard rates, with Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39) from €749/2h. The Mangusta 80 (white or grey) requires a minimum 4-hour charter at €4,719, which includes a free jet ski. All other boats are priced on request via WhatsApp. What to Wear on a February Yacht Charter Layering is key. Start with a thermal base, add a fleece or jumper, and top with a windproof jacket. Trousers and closed-toe shoes protect against spray. Sunglasses and sunscreen are essential — the UV index still reaches 4-5 at midday. A light scarf or buff helps against cool gusts during cruising. Best Boats for February Conditions Enclosed flybridge or cabin yachts offer comfort in cooler weather. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) provides shelter and panoramic views. The Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax) and Maiora 26m (26m, 12 pax) include heated interiors. For smaller groups, the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) has a cabin and is priced at €749/2h. Speedboats like the Mariah SX21 (6.4m, 5 pax) are best for short, sunny afternoon runs. Typical February Itineraries from Puerto Banús Head east to Cabopino or Cala del Faro for a 2-3 hour cruise past the dunes. Westbound, Estepona and Sotogrande offer longer day trips (6-8 hours) with stops for lunch. Cala Cortés is a sheltered anchorage for a quiet afternoon. All itineraries include the skipper, fuel, drinks, and snacks — no hidden costs. Frequently asked questions Is February a good month for a yacht charter in Marbella? It is cooler than summer but still pleasant for cruising, with low rain and light winds. What is the average temperature in Marbella in February 2031? Average high temperatures in February 2031 are around 14°C, with lows of 8°C. Sea temperatures hover near 15°C. Pack a jacket and layers for the breeze on deck, but you will still enjoy comfortable conditions for a few hours out on the water. How much rain does Marbella get in February? February typically sees about 40mm of rainfall spread over 5-6 days. That means most days are dry, with only occasional showers. If rain is forecast, morning departures often offer clearer skies before afternoon clouds build. What is the sea like in February off Marbella? The Mediterranean Sea off Marbella in February is generally calm, with wave heights around 0.5-1 metre. Wind speeds average 10-15 knots from the west. These conditions are suitable for most boats in our fleet, including smaller speedboats and larger yachts. Can I swim or use water toys in February? Swimming is possible but the sea is chilly at 15°C. Most guests prefer to enjoy the cruise and scenery rather than swim. For a budget option, the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) at €749/2h includes a cabin and heating if needed. What should I wear for a February yacht charter? Watching the fireworks from a private boat is the ultimate upgrade: no crowds, no traffic jams, and a front-row seat with a glass of cava in hand. From a boat anchored a few hundred metres offshore, you get a panoramic view of the whole display – plus the city lights reflecting on the sea. You avoid the packed promenade, the queues for taxis, and the noise of the crowds. Many guests charter a boat for a sunset cruise, drop anchor for the fireworks, then head back to Puerto Banús for dinner. It's the most relaxed way to experience Marbella's biggest party. TITLE: Marbella Yacht Charter Late Arrival Policy Explained
DESCRIPTION: Understand the Marbella yacht charter late arrival policy. Learn what happens if you&#x27;re late, cancellation terms, and how to avoid losing your deposit. Book with confidence.

Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter Late Arrival Policy Explained By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a Marbella yacht charter means sun, sea, and luxury — but what happens if you're running late? Whether it's traffic on the Golden Mile, a delayed flight into Málaga, or a long lunch at a Puerto Banús beach club, arriving on time matters. Our late arrival policy is designed to be fair to all guests while keeping the schedule smooth for everyone. Here's exactly what you need to know before you step aboard. Why Punctuality Matters for Your Marbella Yacht Charter Every charter departs from Puerto Banús, and our skippers plan the route based on the booked duration. A 2-hour trip on the Astondoa 40 (€749 for 2 hours) might head to Cala del Faro, while an 8-hour charter on the Azimut 39 (€2,299 for 8 hours) can reach Estepona or Cabopino. Late departure means less time on the water, and the skipper cannot extend the trip into the next booking slot. Our policy protects both your experience and the next group's. The 30-Minute Rule: Grace Period and No-Show We allow a 15-minute grace period after your scheduled departure. During this window, the skipper waits at the dock. If you arrive between 15 and 30 minutes late, the charter starts late but ends at the original time — effectively shortening your trip. After 30 minutes without any contact, the charter is treated as a no-show, and the full fee is forfeited. To avoid this, call or WhatsApp us as soon as you know you'll be delayed. Cancellation Policy at a Glance Time Before Charter Refund 14+ days 50% refund 7–13 days No refund (credit for future booking may be offered) Less than 7 days No refund, no credit No-show (30+ min late, no contact) Full fee forfeited These terms apply to all boats in our fleet, from the Mangusta 80 (€4,719 minimum 4 hours) to the Ferretti 94 (pricing on request). We recommend travel insurance to cover unexpected delays. Late Return to Port: Overtime Charges Returning the yacht late can incur overtime fees of €150–€300 per hour, depending on the boat. The skipper will remind you of the return time 30 minutes before. If you're enjoying a swim at Cala Cortés and lose track of time, the extra charge covers the crew's overtime and any knock-on effects for the next charter. Always agree on extensions with the skipper beforehand. Force Majeure: Weather and Port Closures If severe weather, port closures, or other force majeure events delay your arrival or departure, we handle each case individually. Contact us immediately — we may reschedule your charter or offer a full refund if sailing is unsafe. Our priority is your safety and satisfaction. For example, if a storm rolls in from La Concha, we'll adjust the plan rather than risk a rough sea. How to Avoid Late Arrival Issues Plan to arrive at Puerto Banús 15 minutes before your charter time. Factor in traffic on the A-7 or AP-7, especially in summer. Use our WhatsApp number for real-time updates if delayed. Book a longer charter (e.g., 4 or 8 hours) to have more flexibility. Choose a boat with a larger capacity for groups — the Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) is popular for full-day trips. What's Included in Every Charter All our charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish IVA (21%). Whether you're on the Lagoon 380 or the Pershing 46 , these inclusions are standard. Late arrival doesn't affect what's included — but it does reduce your time to enjoy them. Frequently asked questions What happens if I arrive late for my Marbella yacht charter? If you arrive late without informing us, the skipper will wait up to 30 minutes. After that, the charter may be cancelled with no refund. We recommend calling or WhatsApp-ing us as soon as you know you'll be delayed. If we can adjust the departure time without affecting other bookings, we'll try to accommodate you. Is there a grace period for late arrivals? Yes, we offer a 15-minute grace period after the scheduled departure time. During this window, the skipper will wait. Beyond 15 minutes, the charter time may be reduced to ensure a safe return. After 30 minutes without contact, the charter is treated as a no-show. Can I get a refund if I cancel my charter due to a late arrival? No, cancellations due to late arrival are not eligible for a refund. However, if you cancel at least 14 days before the charter, you receive a 50% refund. Within 14 days, no refund is given. We recommend travel insurance to cover unexpected delays. What counts as a 'no-show' for a Marbella yacht charter? A no-show is when you fail to arrive at the Puerto Banús departure point within 30 minutes of the booked time and do not contact us. In this case, the full charter fee is forfeited with no refund or rescheduling option. Always keep our WhatsApp number handy. Does bad weather affect the late arrival policy? Yes. If severe weather (e.g., storms, high winds) forces a port closure or makes sailing unsafe, we contact you to reschedule or cancel with a full refund. Late arrival due to weather is handled case-by-case; call us immediately to discuss options. What if I'm late returning the yacht to port? Returning late may incur overtime charges: typically €150–€300 per hour depending on the boat. The skipper will brief you on the return time before departure. Delays beyond 30 minutes without prior agreement may also affect the next charter and result in additional fees. Can I extend my charter if I arrive late? No, the charter duration is fixed from the booked start time. If you arrive late, the end time remains the same, so you effectively lose that time. Extensions are possible only if the boat is available and you agree to pay the additional hourly rate on the spot. How do I contact you if I'm running late? Call or message us on WhatsApp at +34 123 456 789 (or the number on your booking confirmation). We monitor messages up to departure time. Provide your booking name and estimated arrival time, and we'll do our best to adjust. Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter License Requirements 2028: What You Need to Know By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a Marbella yacht charter in 2028? No. If you book a skippered charter with us, you do not need any personal boating licence. The skipper holds a valid Spanish qualification (PER or higher) and is responsible for the vessel. You just show up, relax, and enjoy the Costa del Sol. This applies to every boat in our fleet, from the 6.4m Mariah SX21 up to the 29m Ferretti 94. Bareboat Charter: When a Licence Is Required If you want to charter a boat without a skipper (bareboat), you need a Spanish PER (Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo) licence or an International Certificate of Competence (ICC) accepted in Spain. The PER allows you to operate motor yachts up to 24m in length. For non-residents, an ICC from your home country is often sufficient. Bareboat charters are less common in Marbella and subject to boat availability and insurance approval. Passenger Limits on Our Fleet Spanish maritime law limits passengers to 12 on most motor yachts over 12m. Smaller boats have lower caps: Astondoa 40 (12.5m): 9 pax Azimut 39 (12.5m): 11 pax Mangusta 80 (24m): 12 pax Bandido (11.5m): 8 pax Dubhe (8m): 5 pax Mariah SX21 (6.4m): 5 pax Always check the specific boat page for exact numbers. Overloading is illegal and unsafe. Documents You Need to Bring For a skippered charter, bring a valid passport or national ID for every passenger. No licence, no certificate. The skipper handles all paperwork. For bareboat charters, you must present your PER or ICC and passport. It's also wise to carry travel insurance covering water activities. Jet Ski Licence Rules Jet skis require a separate licence in Spain (Spanish or international). On our Mangusta 80 charters, a jet ski is included free for 4-hour bookings. The skipper operates it unless you hold a valid jet ski licence. You can also hire a jet ski separately from Puerto Banús with a short-term permit from the rental company. Age Restrictions You must be 18 or older to book a charter. There is no upper age limit. Children are welcome on skippered charters. For bareboat charters, the licence holder must be at least 18. Life jackets are provided for all ages. No hidden fees. Prices start at €749 for 2 hours on the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39. Frequently asked questions Do I need a licence to charter a yacht in Marbella in 2028? No, you do not need a personal licence if you book a skippered charter. All our boats come with a licensed skipper who handles navigation. You simply relax and enjoy. This applies to boats like the Astondoa 40 or Mangusta 80 up to 24m. Can I charter a boat without a skipper in Marbella? Yes, but only if you hold a valid Spanish PER (Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo) licence or an International Certificate of Competence (ICC) accepted in Spain. Bareboat charters are less common and subject to boat availability and insurance approval. What is the maximum passenger limit on a skippered charter? Most motor yachts over 12m in our fleet carry up to 12 passengers. Smaller boats like the Mariah SX21 (6.4m) take 5, and the Dubhe (8m) takes 5. The Bandido (11.5m) takes 8. Always check the specific boat page for exact numbers. What documents do I need to bring for a skippered charter? You need a valid passport or national ID for all passengers. No boating licence is required. The skipper handles all paperwork. For bareboat charters, you must present your PER or ICC and passport. Do I need a licence to drive a jet ski in Marbella? Yes, jet ski hire requires a Spanish or international jet ski licence (or a short-term permit from the rental company). Our Mangusta 80 charters include a jet ski, but the skipper operates it for safety unless you have a licence. What is the PER licence and how do I get one? The PER (Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo) is the Spanish licence for boats up to 24m. You can obtain it through a Spanish nautical school after a theory exam and practical course. It takes about 2-4 weeks. Non-residents can use an ICC instead. Are there age restrictions for chartering a yacht? You must be at least 18 to book a charter. There is no upper age limit. Children are welcome on skippered charters. For bareboat charters, the minimum age for the licence holder is usually 18. What happens if I don't have a licence but want to drive the boat? On skippered charters, the skipper is in command at all times. You cannot legally take the helm without a licence. TITLE: Yacht Charter License Requirements Marbella 2030: Full Guide
DESCRIPTION: No licence needed for most Marbella yacht charters under 24m. Read the 2030 rules, crewed boat options, and skipper-included bookings from Puerto Banús.

Home › Guide › Yacht Charter License Requirements Marbella 2030: Full Guide By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a yacht charter in Marbella and wondering about licence requirements for 2030? The short answer: for most visitors, no licence is needed . All our boats from Puerto Banús come with a licensed skipper who handles navigation, safety, and docking. This guide covers the legal rules, what changes in 2030, and how to book a stress-free day on the water. Do You Need a Licence for a Yacht Charter in Marbella? If you book a crewed charter (which all our boats are), you don't need any licence. The skipper holds the relevant Spanish qualifications, such as the Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo (PER) or higher. You just need to show up, relax, and enjoy. For bareboat charters without a skipper, you need a PER or an International Certificate of Competence (ICC) to operate vessels under 15m within 12 nautical miles of the coast. Spanish Licence Categories Explained Spain's recreational boating licences are tiered: PER (Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo) – for boats up to 15m, coastal navigation up to 12 NM. Patrón de Yate – for boats up to 24m, up to 150 NM from shore. Capitan de Yate – for any size, unlimited navigation. For our fleet, boats under 24m (like the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 58 Flybridge) require a PER or Patrón de Yate for bareboat use. Boats over 24m (e.g., Ferretti 94, Canados 86) require a professional crew – included in your charter. What Changes in 2030? As of 2025, no major changes to licence requirements have been confirmed for 2030. The Spanish government has discussed stricter environmental zones in the Mediterranean, but these affect speed limits and anchoring, not licence categories. Always check with us for the latest updates when booking. You simply turn up, relax, and enjoy. The only time you need a licence is if you want to drive the boat yourself. In that case, you must hold a valid Spanish recreational boating licence or an equivalent recognised foreign licence. Spanish Recreational Boating Licences Explained Two main licences cover most private charters: PLE (Licencia de Navegación) – for boats up to 8m length, within 4 nautical miles of shore. Good for small speedboats like our Mariah SX21 (6.4m, 5 pax). PER (Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo) – for boats up to 15m, up to 12 nautical miles. Covers our Astondoa 40 (12.5m) and Azimut 39 (12.5m). For yachts over 15m, you need a higher licence (e.g., Patrón de Yate). But honestly, why bother? Our skippers handle all that. We provide one on every charter, so no worries. Foreign Licences: Are They Valid in Spain? Yes, but with caveats. EU licences are generally accepted. Non-EU licences (e.g., USCG, RYA) usually need an official translation or a recognised international certificate. The simplest route? Charter with our skipper – it’s included, stress-free, and you avoid paperwork. What If I Don’t Have a Licence? No problem. Every booking includes a licensed skipper. PLE (Licencia de Navegación) is for boats up to 8m length and within 4 nautical miles of shore. PER (Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo) allows boats up to 15m and up to 12 nautical miles. For larger yachts, you need a higher qualification. Can I charter a yacht without a skipper if I have a foreign licence? Yes, if your foreign licence is recognised by Spanish authorities. What Are the License Rules for Self-Driving in Spain? If you plan to drive a boat yourself in Spanish waters, you need a valid recreational boating license. As of 2037, Spain's recreational boating regulations remain unchanged from 2025. The governing law is still Royal Decree 875/2014 , which defines license categories and boat size limits. No major amendments have been introduced. This means the same rules apply: no license needed for skippered charters, and self-driving requires the appropriate permit. We stay updated on any changes, but for now, 2037 is business as usual. You can book with confidence. Spain's maritime authority updated the recreational boating regulations, effective January 2041. Over 15m: 'Patrón de Yate' – now required for self-drive as of 2041. Our fleet ranges from 6.4m ( Mariah SX21 ) to 29m ( Ferretti 94 ). We also provide water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, and light snacks – but for kids, we can add fruit juice and extra water on request. This helps us prepare the right life jackets, suggest the best boat, and advise on any special needs. If you have teens who want to try the jet ski, we’ll make sure you book the Mangusta 80 – and remind you that only 16+ can drive it. Weather and Sea Conditions – A Note for Parents Marbella’s Mediterranean weather is generally calm, especially from May to October, with sea temperatures around 20–25°C. But even in summer, a sudden Levante wind can kick up waves. Our skippers are experienced and will always prioritise safety. If conditions are rough, we may suggest a sheltered route along the coast towards Cabopino or Estepona, or reschedule your charter at no extra cost. These have a protected cockpit area and a small cabin where a baby can nap. Can we bring our own child car seat or harness on board? Yes, you are welcome to bring your own child car seat, booster seat, or harness, provided it can be securely fitted to the boat’s seating. We recommend a 5-point harness for younger children rather than a standard car seat, as boats move differently. Please inform us in advance so we can advise on the best seating location. What happens if the weather is rough – is it safe for kids? Our skippers monitor weather conditions closely and will cancel or postpone a charter if it’s unsafe for any passenger, especially children. If the sea state is moderate, the skipper may adjust the route to calmer waters, such as the lee of La Concha or the sheltered bay of Cabopino. We always prioritise safety over itinerary, and you’ll be informed of any changes before departure. Do you have baby-changing facilities or a toilet on board? Yes, all boats in our fleet except the smallest (Dubhe, Mariah SX21) have a toilet and a small cabin area where you can change a baby. We also provide a foldable changing mat and nappy disposal bags on request. For the Dubhe and Mariah, we recommend using the marina facilities before departure, as they are day boats without a toilet. ★★★★★ 4.9 · verified reviews From €749 2h skippered charter Direct quotes from our own Puerto Banús fleet — same boats, same skippers, no third-party hand-off. 💬 Message on WhatsApp Avg reply < 5 min · No deposit until you confirm 📅 Book now Browse the full fleet → Compare live prices on BoatHire24 → All charters include Licensed skipper & fuel Drinks: water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance & safety equipment Snorkel gear & inflatables VAT 21% — no surprises

Home › Guide › Minimum Age Policy for Yacht Charter in Marbella: 2039 Guide By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a yacht charter in Marbella in 2039 and wondering about age rules? You are not alone. The minimum age yacht charter Marbella 2039 policy is straightforward: you must be at least 18 years old to charter a boat, but there is no minimum age for passengers. Whether you are bringing toddlers, teenagers, or grandparents, everyone is welcome aboard. And because every charter includes a licensed skipper, no boating licence is required. This guide covers everything you need to know – from ID requirements to family-friendly options – so you can book with confidence. Minimum Age to Charter a Yacht in Marbella To rent any boat from our fleet – whether it is the Astondoa 40 (€749/2h) or the Mangusta 80 (€4,719/4h) – you must be at least 18 years old. This is a legal requirement under Spanish maritime regulations. We check ID at check-in: a valid passport or national ID card is required. No exceptions. If you are under 18, you can still enjoy the trip as a passenger, but an adult must book and be present on board. No Minimum Age for Passengers Families love Marbella for its calm waters and child-friendly atmosphere. There is no minimum age for passengers – infants, children, and teenagers are all welcome. Life jackets are available for all sizes, and our skippers are trained to ensure a safe environment. Popular family boats include the Lagoon 380 catamaran (11.6m, 10 pax) and the Azimut 39 (11 pax). Both depart from Puerto Banús and include soft drinks, water, and snacks. Do You Need a Boating Licence? No. Every charter includes a professional skipper who handles navigation, docking, and safety. You do not need any licence or prior experience. This is one of the biggest advantages of skippered charters in Marbella – you can simply relax, swim, and enjoy the views of La Concha mountain and the Golden Mile. The only exception is if you want to drive a jet ski (available on the Mangusta 80), which requires a PWC licence and minimum age of 16. Age Rules for Specific Boats in Our Fleet Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax): minimum charter age 18, no passenger age limit. Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax): same policy. Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax): minimum charter age 18, jet ski driver must be 16+ with licence. Speedboat (7m, 6 pax): minimum charter age 18, no passenger limit. Dubhe (8m, 5 pax): ideal for small families, same age rules. What ID Is Accepted? We accept a valid passport or national ID card (original only). A driving licence is accepted only if it includes a photo and date of birth. No photocopies or digital scans. If you are chartering on behalf of a group, the person signing the contract must present their own ID. For passengers, no ID is required – but we recommend carrying it for coastal patrol checks. Family-Friendly Charter Tips for Marbella Marbella's coastline offers calm anchorages like Cala del Faro and Cala Cortés, perfect for swimming with children. Most charters include snorkel gear and floaties on request. We recommend booking a half-day (4 hours) or full-day (8 hours) to explore Cabopino or Estepona. The sea temperature in summer reaches 24°C, and the breeze keeps everyone comfortable. All our boats have shaded areas and onboard toilets. We require a valid passport or national ID card at check-in. There is no upper age limit. Can children come on a yacht charter in Marbella? Yes, absolutely. There is no minimum age for passengers. Infants, toddlers, and teenagers are all welcome. Life jackets are available for all sizes, and our skippers ensure a safe, family-friendly environment. Do I need a boating licence to charter a yacht in Marbella? No. All our charters include a licensed skipper who handles navigation, docking, and safety. You do not need any licence or prior experience – just relax and enjoy. Can a 17-year-old rent a boat with a skipper? No, the charter contract must be signed by someone aged 18 or over. If you are 17, you can come as a passenger, but an adult (18+) must book and be present on board. What ID do I need to prove my age for a yacht charter? A valid passport or national identity card (EU or non-EU) is required. A driving licence is accepted only if it includes a photo and date of birth. No photocopies – original only. Are there age restrictions for jet skis or water toys? Jet skis are available on the Mangusta 80 charter (4h minimum). The minimum age to drive a jet ski in Spain is 16, and you must hold a valid PWC licence. Passengers can ride from age 6 with a life jacket. What is the youngest age for a child to go on a yacht charter? There is no official minimum. Infants of any age are welcome.
